# exactCorelib-to-Hyper line audit

This is the persistent workspace-root ledger for the active exactCorelib audit.
The older `exactcore-hyper-comparison/COMPARISON_REPORT.md` is authoritative for
public semantic differential coverage, but it is not evidence that every source
line was reviewed. A file is marked **complete** here only after every line in
the stated range has been read and its ideas have been checked against the
relevant current Hyper implementation.

## Objective and retention order

Audit `exactCorelib-main` file by file and line by line, consult Hyper crates as
needed to understand the scalar and performance architecture, and retain only
measured changes that improve, in order: exactness, completeness, performance,
memory use, binary size, or source size. Correctness and exact boundary behavior
are mandatory gates for every performance experiment.

## Inventory baseline (2026-08-30)

- Subject checkout: `exactCorelib-main`; it has no repository metadata.
- Regular files: 11,975 total; 10,212 under `trunk`, 1,463 under `branches`, and
  292 under the bundled `gmp`, `mpfr`, `mpfi`, and `mpsolve` trees.
- `trunk/inc/CORE` has 37 top-level files and 16,825 physical lines. The four
  implementation translation units in `trunk/src` contain 915 lines.
- The tree also contains binaries/objects/libraries, generated build products,
  CVS administrative files, datasets, papers, and third-party source snapshots.
  They remain in the file inventory. Binary payloads will be classified and
  provenance-checked rather than falsely described as line-readable source;
  third-party source will be audited in its own phase so its lineage is not
  confused with exactCore's implementation.

## File-by-file ledger

| File and reviewed range | Status | Hyper disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Config.h:1-58` | complete | Compile-time namespace/debug/refcount/mempool switches. Rust feature/configuration mechanisms already provide the applicable separation; no exactness or performance idea to transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/RefCounting.h:1-60` | complete | Non-atomic intrusive reference counting plus copy-on-write. Hyper already uses immutable `Arc` DAG nodes and an at-most-48-byte `Real`; replacing value certificates with mandatory heap-indirected handles would trade thread safety and allocation cost for handle size. Rejected without experiment because it weakens required semantics and contradicts measured hot rational construction. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/SupportT.h:1-119` | complete | Uniform trig adapter across numeric levels, backed by a mutable translation-unit precision default outside level 3. Hyper's explicit exact/computable APIs already cover `pi`, `sin`, `cos`, and `atan` without a process-global precision downgrade. The public `cot` completeness question is carried below. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Policies.h:1-170` | complete | Raw/fixed/automatic precision dispatch for MPFR-backed operations. Hyper keeps requested precision explicit in `Computable::approx` and uses Rust types instead of runtime global precision; no transfer. This file exposes `exp2`, `exp10`, and `cot`, recorded as an API-completeness candidate below. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Promote.h:1-192` | complete | Runtime capability queries and a closed promotion table. Rust trait/type inference already supplies the useful compile-time behavior. `hasExactDivision<BigRat>` merely confirms Hyper's existing exact-rational division contract. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/BFInterval.h:1-22` | complete | Lightweight `(BigFloat, BigFloat)` root-isolation intervals with reversed endpoints as an error sentinel. HyperSolve already uses typed isolating intervals/results; adopting an invalid numeric sentinel would reduce exactness and diagnosability. Rejected. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/CoreDefs.h:1-248` | complete | Process-global precision, cutoff, weak-BigFloat, and error state plus decimal/binary precision conversion. Hyper uses explicit bounded certification policies and typed uncertainty. `SignCondition` anticipates the richer sign/domain certificates already implemented by HyperReal/HyperLimit. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Filters.h:1-278` | complete | Burnikel-Funke-Schirra-style propagated binary64 sign filter (`value`, magnitude bound, operation index) with exact fallback. Hyper already has conservative retained rational linear-form filters, structural facts, dyadic intervals, and arbitrary-precision fallback. The generic expression-level filter is a possible performance experiment only if later profile evidence identifies repeated cold `Computable` sign refinement; it is not safe to insert speculatively. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Gmpz.h:1-127` | complete | Thin GMP integer owner plus optional non-atomic intrusive copy-on-write. Hyper's `BigInt`/`BigUint` owner types and immutable `Arc<RationalData>` already provide safe ownership and shared rational clones. No algorithm to transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Gmpq.h:1-136` | complete | Thin GMP rational owner with eager canonicalization at selected constructors and optional intrusive copy-on-write. Hyper's rational layer already provides thread-safe sharing, typed zero-denominator failure, small-value interning, and lazy internal canonicalization. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/BigInt.h:1-1287` | complete | GMP forwarding surface: word-specialized arithmetic, truncating and floor division variants, exact division, factor removal, shifts, comparisons, bit operations, and COW mutation. Hyper already uses word/fixed-limb dispatch, dyadic shifts, mixed-width GCD, cross-cancellation, and ownership recovery. Source review found exactCore hazards that must not be copied: signed-minimum negation in word overloads; `pow(long,unsigned long)` tests base parity instead of exponent parity at line 336; `cmpabs(double)` does not take the argument's absolute value at line 411; and the static random state at lines 1169-1181 is unsynchronized. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/BigRat.h:1-855` | complete | GMP rational arithmetic, canonicalization, direct power-of-two scaling, comparison, and conversions. Hyper's current implementation is strictly richer on the applicable paths (cross-cancelled arithmetic, dyadic reduction, lazy canonicalization, exact conversions, caches, typed errors). Source hazards not to copy: string assignment omits canonicalization at lines 89-94 although construction performs it; `reciprocal` swaps limbs without zero/sign normalization at lines 217-221; and integer conversions route through lossy binary64 at lines 193-206. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Mpfr.h:1-171` | complete | Minimal RAII wrapper plus optional non-atomic intrusive COW around an MPFR value. Precision and default rounding remain ambient process state, and string construction discards MPFR's parse result. Hyper's immutable expression graph, explicit requested precision, and typed parse failures are stronger; no ownership or rounding mechanism to transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/BigFloat.inl:1-750` | complete | Mostly symmetric arithmetic/comparison overload forwarding, stream formatting, and exact-dyadic divisibility/GCD helpers. Applicable power-of-two scaling is already native to Hyper's dyadic representation. Hazards not to copy include an EOF-unaware file scan at lines 598-610, signed overflow in `minStar`'s `m*n` sign test at lines 589-596, and unsigned precision subtraction before conversion in `div_exact` at lines 668-676. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/BigFloat.h:1-2074` | complete | MPFR-backed raw/fixed/automatic arithmetic, directed rounding, transcendental wrappers, exact float-to-rational extraction, and heuristic precision sizing. The exposed `exp2`/`exp10` completeness idea is retained at Hyper's exact `Real` layer; direct MPFR cotangent leaves the public-cot experiment open but contributes no algorithm. Hyper must not copy several correctness hazards: automatic `long` precision is only `sizeof(int)*8` at lines 100-105 and 1771-1776; exact rational arguments are pre-rounded to 53 bits at lines 112-114 and 1783-1785; the generic `div(T,T,prec,rnd)` drops both `prec` and `rnd` at lines 756-761; signed `sqrt(long)` relies on a debug assertion before conversion to unsigned at lines 1221-1226; the in-place fixed `root` branches pass the rounding enum as the root degree and discard `k` at lines 1429-1448; and automatic add/multiply precision uses narrowing casts and unchecked arithmetic at lines 1795-1851. Explicit certified dyadic enclosures remain the better Hyper contract. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/BigFloat2.h:1-1271` | complete | Two directed MPFR endpoints plus an exact-point flag, routed through the arithmetic policies. Hyper already exposes certified dyadic enclosures without storing an interval in every scalar. Several public operations weaken proof semantics: `long` construction inherits BigFloat's 32-bit precision yet marks the result exact at lines 73-76; `set_prec` destructively resets endpoint values at lines 138-148; `get_z`/`get_q` silently return the lower endpoint and `get_f`/`centerize` replace an enclosure with a midpoint at lines 712-907; interval sign uncertainty is conflated with exact zero at lines 909-931; and `uMSB`/`lMSB` select fixed endpoints even for negative or zero-crossing intervals at lines 932-937. The extra endpoint storage and mutable exactness bit offer no improvement over Hyper's on-demand typed certificates. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/BigFloat2.inl:1-674` | complete | Directed-endpoint implementations for assignment, elementary functions, and four arithmetic operations. The monotone add/subtract/multiply core is a useful checklist already covered by Hyper's exact dyadic interval tests, but this implementation has containment failures that must not transfer: string exactness converts `cmp` directly to `bool`, reversing equal/different at lines 40-49; inexact `set_2exp` writes the upward-rounded result back into `m_l` and leaves `m_r` stale at lines 57-69; `sin`, `cos`, `tan`, and `cot` map only endpoints without extrema/pole reduction at lines 157-240, while cosine also reverses point-rounding bounds; decreasing `acos` maps endpoints in increasing order at lines 263-282; exact-interval plus scalar can read undefined `x.m_r` after an inexact lower result at lines 447-456; and exact numerator divided by a zero-containing interval bypasses the full-interval guard at lines 599-674. Rejected as a transfer source. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/IntervalT.h:1-466` | complete | Generic endpoint arithmetic and an incomplete extended-division API. It neither validates endpoint order nor directs machine/BigFloat rounding outward, so it is not a certified interval abstraction. Additional hazards include self-subtraction alias corruption at lines 169-173, denominator-zero division becoming a no-op in release builds at lines 175-193, binary64 constants in generic expansion at lines 195-212, finite integer sentinels standing in for infinity at lines 232-244, and incomplete zero/zero cases in extended division at lines 415-458. Hyper's typed on-demand enclosures and explicit uncertainty are strictly stronger; no transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/ComplexT.h:1-414` | complete | Cartesian and polar complex wrappers used by a root-isolation program. Cartesian `arg` implements `atan(im/re)` with an incomplete correction rather than `atan2`, giving wrong third/fourth-quadrant and imaginary-axis results at lines 78-89. Polar construction fails to normalize negative angles, accepts negative moduli, and performs repeated subtraction for large angles at lines 174-205; conversion therefore compounds the argument defect. Real equality also references `mod` rather than `mod_` at lines 296-309. HyperReal is deliberately real-only, so complex arithmetic is out of scalar scope; these wrappers supply no safe reusable kernel. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/CoreAux.h:1-439` | complete | Bit-log/GCD adapters, diagnostic helpers, and decimal significant-digit compatibility parsing. Hyper already uses integer bit lengths and exact decimal/scientific parsing with typed exhaustion. Hazards not to copy include signed-minimum negation and zero divisors in primitive helpers at lines 81-123; NaN classified as negative by `sgn(double)` at lines 141-152; unchecked empty/all-zero string indexing and `atoi` exponent truncation in `getUncertainty`/`getDigits` at lines 202-275; unbounded decimal exponent materialization at lines 277-298; and a strict comparison in `isCompatibleEfficient` that contradicts the documented closed-interval overlap rule at lines 378-403. Implicit decimal uncertainty is also the wrong contract for Hyper's exact parser. |
| `trunk/src/CoreAux.cpp:1-107` | complete | Primitive bit-log/GCD calls are forwarded through heap-capable `BigInt`; diagnostics mutate unsynchronized globals, append to a process-relative file, and terminate the process at lines 79-104. Hyper's `Result`/`Problem` propagation and caller-controlled diagnostics are safer and composable. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/CoreIO.h:1-180` | complete | Iostream formatting adapters plus process-global rounding/base controls. A nonstandard base set on one stream changes formatting of every stream, and the rounding mode is ambient unsynchronized state. The compatibility helper also couples presentation precision to global computation precision. Hyper's formatting and approximation requests are value/call scoped; no transfer. |
| `trunk/src/CoreIo.cpp:1-482` | complete | Experimental large-integer token/file I/O with manual buffers; more than one third of the file is disabled legacy code. Active hazards include unchecked doubling/size conversions at lines 60-93, an infinite comment scan when EOF precedes newline at lines 95-115, inline `#` comments not actually discarding the rest of the line at lines 136-159, negative/truncated lengths and binary64 digit sizing at lines 161-229, zero `charsPerLine` nontermination at lines 231-257, and `sizeof(pointer)` passed as token capacity at lines 259-278. Errors abort the process. Hyper's bounded parser, owned strings, and typed failures already cover the useful contract. |
| `trunk/src/MpfrIO.cpp:1-189` | complete | MPFR parsing through an intermediate `mpf` and custom formatting via private MPFR/GMP representation fields. The input path can double-round; the exponent formatter uses a shared 32-byte static buffer, narrows the exponent to `int`, and is not thread-safe at lines 55-73. `mpfr2str` has unchecked size/exponent negation, potentially enormous forced-fixed padding, ignores `showpoint`, only partly honors uppercase, and feeds bases above 16 to a 16-digit `itoa` alphabet at lines 90-187. No transfer; Hyper's exact round-trip and bounded lossy formatting should remain independent of MPFR internals. |
| `trunk/src/CoreDefs.cpp:1-137` | complete | Definitions for all mutable process-wide precision/filter/error/test state. `coreEnv::apply` unexpectedly prints on first use and leaks a global stack; `restoreEnv` dereferences that pointer and its top element before checking null/emptiness at lines 89-133. The environment is not thread-local. This confirms the earlier rejection of ambient precision/error state. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/README:1-20` | complete | Short 2006 design inventory; it describes the then-planned filter/kernel/interval split but is stale relative to the checked-in BigFloat/BigFloat2 classes. Hyper already has an explicit structural-fact/filter/computable separation. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/TODO:1-114` | complete | Historical incomplete-work list and a macro sketch for separating filter, sign, MSD, approximation, and root-bound rules. Hyper's typed node representation already separates these concerns without source-generating macros; its `Class`/`Irrational` distinction also covers the proposed non-algebraic flag. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/CORE.h:1-114` | complete | Umbrella header that selects numeric levels by globally redefining `long`, `double`, `BigInt`, `BigFloat`, and `Expr`, then injects the CORE namespace. Rust traits/features provide specialization without lexical type substitution or include-order dependence. Rejected for correctness, diagnostics, and code-size hygiene. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Timer.h:1-83` | complete | CPU-time helpers with millisecond resolution, `float` result conversion, a misleading microseconds comment, and a Windows implementation that always returns zero. Criterion's statistical wall/CPU controls are substantially stronger; no transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/geombase.h:1-49` | complete | Minimal polymorphic geometry base and exception tags. It has no scalar algorithm; Hyper geometry crates use typed Rust errors and do not need a shared virtual root object. The subdirectory geometry implementations are closed separately below and produced no reason to adopt this shared virtual root. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/geometry2d.h:1-53` | complete | Include/autolink umbrella only. The referenced `geom2d` sources are closed separately in this ledger; this file contributes no algorithm or scalar policy. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/geometry3d.h:1-63` | complete | Include/autolink umbrella only. The referenced `geom3d` and linear-algebra sources are not considered audited by reading this file. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/VExpr.h:1-86` | complete | Unfinished vector-expression facade. It shares `Expr.h`'s include guard, refers to an undeclared/non-templated `VExprRep`, lacks the pointer constructor/destructor needed by `rootOf`, and therefore cannot implement its advertised ownership path. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/VExprRep.h:1-76` | complete | Unfinished intrusive non-atomic DAG prototype with the same include guard as `ExprRep.h`; its bivariate interface constructor ignores both polynomials and hard-codes `(x,y)=(1,0)`. Hyper's immutable `Arc` DAG is complete and thread-safe. Rejected. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/ExprRep.inl:1-26` | complete | Contains only two polynomial/Sturm includes; there are no inline expression algorithms to transfer. The included subdirectory files are closed in the polynomial ledger below and produced no separate representation change. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/RootBounds.h:1-867` | complete | Four expression separation-bound trackers propagate algebraic degree/height and optionally factor powers of two and five out of constants. The high-level idea—compute algebraic nonzero-separation metadata on demand so sign refinement has a finite sufficient precision—is a genuine exactness/completeness candidate recorded below, but these implementations are not safe to copy. Reused zero assignments leave stale fields; zero degrees/root orders underflow; degree, valuation, and height arithmetic is unchecked; floating variants subtract upward-rounded terms from an intended upper bound at lines 104-109 and 363-375; the integer variant admits its `log2(5)` rounding is not exact at lines 567-585; `BfmssRootBd` leaves `u_e/l_e` uninitialized at lines 759-786; and `MinRootBd` forwards whole wrapper objects where component bounds are required at lines 838-863. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Expr.h:1-816` | complete | Public intrusive DAG handle, arithmetic/transcendental construction, polynomial-root leaves, n-ary reductions, comparisons, approximation, and conversions. The useful separation of cheap filters, lazy approximation, and on-demand root metadata is already present in Hyper as compact symbolic classes, structural facts, shared computable nodes, certified dyadic enclosures, and process-wide approximation caches. Hyper also already uses exponentiation by squaring, whereas `power(e,k)` materializes O(|k|) repeated children and negates `LONG_MIN` at lines 410-429. Correctness hazards rule out direct reuse: a `Kernel` interval is collapsed through `get_f()` and relabeled dyadic at line 164; the source itself says templated `sqrt` can return a wrong result at lines 264-271; public `tan`/`cot` ignore their dedicated nodes and construct quotients at lines 326-334; the large-argument `acos` recurrence moves toward 1 and recursively calls the same branch, so it is both mathematically wrong and nonterminating at lines 350-361; unchecked inclusive range loops can wrap at lines 439-455; rational reduction prints to stdout; parsing ignores stream failure and exact-base arguments; scalar conversions silently choose approximations/endpoints; the selected double root-bound policy is explicitly not overflow-proof; `gcd` is a constant-one stub and `div_exact` returns an approximation at lines 776-787; and `floorLg`/`ceilLg` have no valid zero/negative base case. No additional transfer survives this review; exactCore's cot surface remains only an API prompt, not an implementation source. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/ExprRep.h:1-2424` | complete | Full expression-node implementation: lazy `NodeInfo`, BFS filter, cached sign/MSB/enclosure, constructive refinement, constant/radical/transcendental/binary/n-ary nodes, and intrusive ownership. Its lazy side allocation supports Hyper's existing decision not to burden every scalar with separation metadata, but the implementation violates the exactness contract in core paths. When an escape or cutoff bound is exhausted, `refine` warns and unconditionally caches exact zero at lines 452-523; the algebraic branch can leave `bound_type` indeterminate and degree/precision arithmetic is unchecked. Base approximation virtuals mutually recurse if neither is overridden at lines 616-624; unsupported exact-value conversions silently return zero at lines 714-759; one polynomial-root constructor leaves `opType` unset at lines 870-885; `ACosRepT` claims every result is positive even at `acos(1)=0` at lines 1399-1413; exponential MSB formulas contain an unreachable negative branch, and base-10 uses a factor two where `log2(10)>3`, invalidating magnitude bounds at lines 1491-1631; logarithmic MSB rules take bit-logs of signed child-MSB bounds; radical formulas mix signed MSBs with unsigned degrees and multiply precision unchecked at lines 1817-1841; `MulRepT::getQTVal` prints and reevaluates both children repeatedly; and n-ary sum/product methods index empty vectors, overflow accumulated bounds, and omit product `numType` updates on insertion at lines 2139-2344. Hyper's typed `Unknown`/`Exhausted` outcomes, conservative structural certificates, checked resource bounds, `Arc` ownership, and enclosure caches are strictly stronger. The only retained architectural question remains a newly derived, checked separation bound for a narrowly classified algebraic subset. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/Wrappers.h:1-979` | complete | Experimental API-compatibility wrappers around `long`, `int`, and `double`, mostly repetitive mixed-type forwarding. They deliberately ignore approximation precision and have no certified semantics. Integer powers round through binary64 before narrowing at lines 61-64 and 169-174; string parsing does not check failure and can leave `DoubleWrapper::d_val` indeterminate at lines 343-351; `lMSB()` is a constant-zero stub at lines 371-374; signed integer operations inherit overflow UB while mixed unsigned overloads silently change conversion domains; division exposes raw IEEE infinities/NaNs; and `sign(NaN)` returns zero at lines 930-938. Rust's primitive traits and Hyper's explicit exact/lossy boundaries already provide the intended source compatibility without wrapper bloat or false certificates. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/linearAlgebra.h:1-177` | complete | Declaration-only raw-array vector/matrix API. Its apparent `double` scalar is rewritten by `CORE.h` macros at higher numeric levels, coupling representation to include order; dimensions are signed integers and storage ownership is manual. The algorithms in `trunk/ext/linearAlgebra.cpp` are closed separately below and add unchecked dimension/index and singularity hazards. HyperLattice's typed fixed-size matrices/vectors and HyperSolve's dynamic solve reports already expose safer boundaries, so this surface contributes no standalone candidate. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/linearAlgebraT.h:1-887` | complete | Generic raw-array vectors/matrices plus cofactor determinants and a fraction-free Bareiss inverse/determinant. HyperSolve already has dense, multi-RHS, sparse, minimum-degree, and pattern-preserving Bareiss implementations with typed dimension/pivot/division/residual evidence, so the high-level algorithm is not new. This implementation is not safe to reuse: negative dimensions and `int` dimension products reach unchecked allocations; `maxnorm` always returns zero at lines 307-313; unary negation hard-codes a 3-vector at lines 344-347; `cross` logs but continues out of bounds and the variadic generalized cross leaks its pointer array at lines 372-412; `isIdentity` repeatedly indexes `_rep[i]` rather than `(i,j)` at lines 532-543; non-square transpose leaks the old buffer at lines 590-599; Bareiss pivoting rejects the last candidate before testing it, loses column-permutation/sign metadata, and then returns a fixed physical column at lines 612-734; cofactor extraction uses `dim1` as the row stride at lines 750-771; and zero-size determinant underflows `n-1`. No Hyper change or benchmark is justified from this version. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/poly/PolyBase.h:1-208` | complete | Dense coefficient owner with optional intrusive copy-on-write scaffolding. HyperSolve's polynomial APIs already use owned vectors and validated shape. Here the vector constructor ignores its nominal-degree argument and narrows `size()-1`; the placeholder string constructor leaves both fields indeterminate; assignment and `set` leak the old coefficient buffer when the new degree is `-1`; the vector setter contains an invalid `vector != 0` assertion; and `RcPolyBase` reaches private `PolyBase` fields directly at lines 197-202. The preprocessor selection is internally inconsistent as well: defining `CORE_DISABLE_REFCOUNTING` selects `RcPolyBase`, whose declaration is disabled by that same macro. No ownership/layout idea transfers. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/poly/PolyFun.h:1-208` | complete | Generic/approximate Horner evaluation, a filtered sign fallback, Cauchy upper bound, and Mobius transforms. Horner evaluation itself is standard and already pervasive in HyperSolve. The interval path inherits `BigFloat2`'s containment failures, ignores its absolute-precision argument, and turns the forbidden `BigInt` specialization into `assert(0)` followed by zero. Precision heuristics can overflow and negate the minimum signed value; the fallback depends on exactCore's unsound expression zero assertion; and the supposedly certified non-filter path can wrap an already-rounded scalar as an interval. HyperSolve's exact coefficient evaluation and explicit refinement policies are stronger. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/poly/Poly.h:1-2059` | complete | Dense polynomial parsing/arithmetic, pseudo-division/GCD, resultants, composition, root/height bounds, and interval forms. HyperSolve already implements the applicable exact polynomial, square-free, Sturm/Descartes, resultant/subresultant, and bounded-root packages with checked dimensions and typed evidence. This source is not a safe algorithm donor. Parsing allocates no room for terminators, scans past unmatched parentheses, writes a newline rather than a NUL before `atoi`, reports errors but continues, and uses an always-true variable-name condition at lines 43-300. Zero contraction/differentiation leak through `PolyBase`; zero `reverse()` indexes null; negative powers become enormous unsigned loops; `prem` attempts to assign through a const reference and pseudo-remainder initializes `C` by leaking a heap object at lines 657-750. The reverse formatter drops the sign of a leading negative term; input trusts negative/unbounded degrees; internal test code does not compile; `primPart` does not enforce its promised positive leading coefficient; and linear `sqFreePart` returns the input rather than unit gcd. Most decisively, resultant constant base cases return only a signed coefficient rather than the required coefficient power at lines 1531-1546; `disc` explicitly omits its defining sign/leading-coefficient factor; multiplicity factoring leaks and fails on low degrees. Iterative bounds crash on the zero polynomial; Smale's test chooses lower rather than upper magnitudes for negative enclosures and multiplies by a lower height bound, allowing false acceptance; `yapsBound` converts degree `-1` to unsigned; `length` omits the leading coefficient; and coefficient sign variation indexes `-1` for zero. No new Hyper candidate survives. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/poly/Sturm.h:1-1185` | complete | Sturm-sequence construction, sign-variation counts, interval isolation, bisection, and Newton/Smale refinement. HyperSolve already has exact Sturm isolation and separately certified Smale analysis. This implementation has lifetime and instantiation failures before its numerical issues: default/zero construction can leave `seq` indeterminate while the destructor deletes it, the raw-array constructor assigns through uninitialized state, copying omits `g` and `cont`, infinity variation both refers to raw `coeff` storage and can omit the final sequence member, and pure refinement calls a nonexistent polynomial member. Its Newton error term is not a rounding-error bound: it constructs a fresh rounded copy of the correction and later adds it to that correction, approximately doubling the step; negative remaining-root counts can escape exhaustion handling; signed precision negation can overflow; and the duplicated Smale test uses the same wrong endpoint and lower-height choices as `Poly.h`. The final interval therefore lacks a valid certificate. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/poly/Descartes.h:1-971` | complete | Recursive Descartes isolation plus optional Newton refinement. HyperSolve already supplies a checked Descartes report/pruning pipeline and bounded Sturm fallback. Here an early sign-count return leaks its temporary, every recursive unit-interval call leaks two coefficient buffers (exponential aggregate leakage), zero polynomials reach invalid degrees, and generic paths use nonstandard variable-length arrays. The default constructor leaves `len` indeterminate; copying omits the cached derivative, so copied Newton refinement can operate on zero; setting zero leaves stale polynomial state; direct root lookup can walk off the interval vector; output vectors are not consistently cleared; and negative roots are emitted in reverse physical order. The Newton path inherits Sturm's invalid correction/error certificate, while its decisive post-refinement verification is compiled only under `CORE_DEBUG`; release builds can accept a wrong or overly wide interval. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/poly/README:1-16` | complete | Historical timing note comparing the two root isolators, with later source comments contradicting its ordering and no reproducible workload, environment, or raw measurements. It is not benchmark evidence for HyperSolve. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/poly/Curves.h:1-954` | complete | Public monomial/bivariate-polynomial/curve declarations and a root-isolator adapter. HyperCurve and HyperSolve already provide typed bivariate polynomials, resultants, curve analysis, and exact isolators. This header exports `using namespace std`, leaves monomial state indeterminate by default, exposes mutable degree/coefficient invariants, permits negative color indices, and gives `RootIsolator` an implicitly copyable owning raw pointer (double-delete risk) plus an unused reference that can dangle. Root-isolator choice is hardwired rather than evidence-driven. No transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/poly/Curves.tcc:1-1900` | complete | Bivariate parsing, evaluation, arithmetic, pseudo-remainder GCD/resultants, specialization to univariate polynomials, vertical isolation, and heuristic plotting. The parser repeats `Poly.h`'s out-of-bounds terminator, unmatched-parenthesis, nonterminating token, and always-true variable-validation defects. Constructors accept inconsistent negative degrees; query methods mutate representation; several evaluators and plotting buffers use nonstandard or unbounded stack VLAs; expression evaluation expands separate powers rather than Horner; and zero operands/divisors reach negative allocation sizes or invalid leading-coefficient indices. `replaceYwithX` declares a stack VLA and then calls `delete[]` on it at lines 1327-1339. The bivariate resultant warns its sign may be wrong and indexes the pseudo-quotient at degree `-1` before checking whether the remainder vanished at lines 1451-1489. Vertical isolation first rounds coefficients to `BigFloat` and then routes through exactCore's already-unsound interval/refinement stack; plotting accepts unchecked file indices and nonpositive steps, narrows interval counts/sizes, and allocates a runtime-sized two-dimensional stack buffer. Hyper's existing exact bivariate/resultant/root stack is both broader and safer; no candidate survives. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/geom2d/point2d.h:1-166` | complete | Declaration surface for exact-coordinate points, orientation, signed double-area, midpoint, and strict betweenness. The scalar spelled `double` is globally rewritten to `Expr` at the forced default `CORE_LEVEL 3`, making the API depend on macro/include order. HyperTri already has typed exact orientation and degeneracy evidence; implementation bodies remain in the separately inventoried extension file. No standalone transfer. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/geom2d/line2d.h:1-156` | complete | Directed two-point line declarations with distance/projection, orientation, coincidence, parallelism, and heap-polymorphic intersection. HyperTri's determinant-first predicates and typed intersection results avoid those surface hazards. The implementation is closed in the extension ledger and confirms rather than repairs the degenerate/ownership problems; no candidate survives. |
| `trunk/inc/CORE/geom2d/segment2d.h:1-175` | complete | Open/closed and directed segment declaration with containment/intersection. `setOpen(bool)` ignores its argument and assigns `directed = open` at line 89, corrupting the unrelated flag while leaving openness unchanged. HyperTri already models segment degeneracy and typed intersections; no transfer. |

HyperTri already has exact incircle/circumcenter certificates with explicit degeneracy. The implementation is closed in the extension ledger and preserves these hazards; no candidate survives. |

Hyper already has explicit primitive kinds and degeneracy/result types. No transfer. |

The implementation must be checked for invariant preservation, but the surface offers nothing missing from HyperTri/HyperPlane-style exact predicates. |

This is allocation-heavy and invariant-unsafe compared with contiguous Rust ownership. Rejected. |

The implementation is closed in the extension ledger and supplies no safer degeneracy or ownership contract; no candidate survives. |

The parser uses ctype on unchecked `char`, can inspect an uninitialized/stale character after failed reads, treats malformed/EOF states as permission to extract, assigns a point without validating both coordinates, truncates comments at 255 characters, and `readPoints` ignores extraction failure and pointer/capacity validity. `aCenter` also injects a machine-double weight into otherwise exact coordinates. No transfer. |
| `trunk/ext/geom2d/line2d.cpp:1-171` | complete | Two-point/equation construction, projection, distance, orientation, and line intersection. The coefficient constructor divides by zero for the all-zero equation and degeneracy is guarded only by assertions. More decisively, `intersects` checks parallelism before coincidence, so coincident lines always return disjoint; its coincidence branch is unreachable at lines 100-108. The separately defined `orientation2d(Line2d&,Point2d&)` does not match the header's const-reference friend declaration, leaving the advertised overload undefined. Vertical slope reports an error and returns the valid-looking value zero, parsing ignores stream failures, and every concrete intersection is a caller-owned raw allocation. Hyper's determinant classifications are stronger; no transfer. |
| `trunk/ext/geom2d/segment2d.cpp:1-218` | complete | Distance/projection, containment, intersection classification, and raw-object construction. Every constructor—including the copy constructor—forces both flags false, losing the source segment's open/directed semantics. Degenerate segments reach asserted line construction and division by zero. Rejected intersection points leak whenever later containment fails; collinear construction loses endpoint openness and direction; and open-segment distance/nearest-point semantics are explicitly left unresolved. Input unexpectedly writes prompts to stdout. HyperTri already has typed degeneracy/intersection evidence and value ownership. No transfer. |
| `trunk/ext/geom2d/circle2d.cpp:1-177` | complete | Circumcircle/incircle determinant and lazy center/radius computation, but the object model is catastrophically unsafe. The default constructor leaves `orient`, `cp`, and `rp` indeterminate for its destructor; the declared one-point and copy constructors are absent; assignment leaks old caches, shallow-copies new ones, and causes double deletion. The center/radius constructor calls nonmutating `rotate90` twice and discards both returned points, so all three defining points remain identical and every determinant-side test returns zero. Collinear triples terminate the process despite the header's promised degenerate-line representation; `center()` can dereference a null intersection; and extraction takes its circle by value. HyperTri's exact incircle/circumcenter implementation is already safe and certified. Rejected. |
| `trunk/ext/geom3d/Makefile:1-22` | complete | Like the 2D counterpart, this defines cleanup recursion but no build target or algorithm. No transfer. |
| `trunk/ext/geom3d/point3d.cpp:1-170` | complete | Point arithmetic, Euclidean distance, exact determinant volume/orientation, and stream parsing. HyperTri already provides filtered exact 3D orientation. The vector constructor's dimension check is reversed: it rejects valid vectors longer than three, accepts shorter vectors, and then indexes their missing coordinates at lines 49-51. Parsing again uses unchecked ctype/read state and can assign failed extractions. The raw determinant itself is conventional and not new. |
| `trunk/ext/geom3d/line3d.cpp:1-109` | complete | Cross-product distance/containment, coplanarity classification, projection, and line intersection. Proper-line invariants are never enforced; zero direction divides by zero and makes `contains` accept every point. Intersection returns raw owning allocations, and extraction writes interactive prompts to stdout. The exact algebra is standard and already implemented with typed degeneracy in HyperTri; no transfer. |
| `trunk/ext/geom3d/plane3d.cpp:1-258` | complete | Plane construction, containment, projection, distances, and intersections contain multiple decisive semantic failures. The point-plus-line and point-plus-segment constructors construct and discard a temporary, leaving the actual coefficient fields uninitialized. `coeffients()` writes all four values into slot zero and returns three indeterminate slots. `isCoincident` recognizes nonparallel normals with equal raw displacement rather than proportional plane equations. Distance treats integer intersection dimensions as booleans, returning zero for disjoint (`-1`) lines/segments and a positive endpoint distance for a proper crossing (`0`). Contained-line intersection divides by a zero normal-direction dot product instead of returning a line; rejected segment points and the plane-plane helper leak allocations; parallel coincident planes return null. No result or kernel can safely transfer. |
| `trunk/ext/geom3d/polygon3d.cpp:1-277` | complete | Circular doubly linked polygon implementation with per-node and per-point allocation. Negative indices pass its assertion and alias the head; empty value removal dereferences null; iterator removal (from the header) desynchronizes size/head; self-assignment erases the polygon; and equality aborts or dereferences null on small polygons. `verify` rejects valid three-vertex polygons, accepts no edge/self-intersection checks, and can build its plane from collinear leading vertices. Output sends vertices to global stdout rather than the supplied stream. Contiguous typed storage in Hyper is smaller, faster, and safer. Rejected. |
| `trunk/ext/geom3d/segment3d.cpp:1-254` | complete | 3D analogue of the flawed 2D segment code, with an additional destructive bug: `reverse` assigns `p0=p1` and then `p1=p0`, collapsing both endpoints onto the old stop point at lines 71-76. Exact dot/orientation primitives already exist in HyperTri. No transfer. |
| `trunk/ext/geom3d/triangle3d.cpp:1-438` | complete | Triangle containment, clipping, and pairwise intersection are built on exact determinants but misuse their geometry and ownership. Containment treats the normal vector as an absolute viewpoint rather than `p0+normal`, does not require coplanarity, and accepts a point whenever any one edge orientation is zero, including edge-line extensions. Coplanar `do_intersect` methods test dimension codes as booleans, so disjoint `-1` is true. Every `intersects` query allocates an intersection and leaks it. Plane slicing contains unreachable deletes after returns and can dereference a null second point; rejected points leak. Coplanar segment clipping returns a pointer owned by a soon-destroyed local polygon, while repeated `in_half_plane` results and line-edge points leak. Its coplanar orientation repeats the absolute-normal-point mistake. HyperTri/HyperCurve already have certified orientation, clipping, and typed intersection ownership; no candidate survives. |
| `trunk/ext/linearAlgebra.cpp:1-556` | complete | Macro-selected raw-array vectors/matrices, norms, dot/cross products, Gaussian determinant, cofactors, transpose, and multiplication. This is an older and weaker duplicate of `linearAlgebraT.h`, not a donor for HyperLattice or HyperSolve. Signed negative dimensions and products reach allocation; copying a default `dim=-1` vector allocates a huge array; `maxnorm` always returns zero while `infnorm` is actually the one-norm; unary negation hard-codes dimension three; cross product logs a dimension error and continues out of bounds; and generalized cross leaks its pointer array. Rectangular transpose leaks the old matrix buffer, element access has its range checks commented out, and the determinant explicitly marked “not right” loses the sign of row permutations and builds division-heavy expression graphs. Non-square minors use `dim1` rather than `dim2` as the source stride. HyperSolve's checked Bareiss variants and HyperLattice's typed fixed-size operations already cover every sound operation with better exactness and ownership. No transfer. |

It also labels C14's chosen precision heuristic, relies on an unproved derivative-maximum conjecture for C22, and shows unstable generic matrix inversion losing to a closed-form exact formula in C23. These support certified node-local refinement and symbolic structure, not replacing Hyper's exact semantics with fixed-precision recipes. |

| `trunk/progs/curves/localCurves.h:1-1386` | complete-broken | Experimental pre-library bivariate polynomial and plotting implementation, superseded by the already-audited official `CORE/poly/Curves.*`. It does not parse as current C++: in-class operators carry illegal extra qualification, `core_min` calls are unresolved, and `differentiateXY` asserts undeclared `i`/`j`. More fundamentally, its string constructors create discarded temporaries instead of initializing `this`; empty input does the same. Dense construction allocates `max` elements then writes indices through `max`, indexes the flattened coefficient input incorrectly, and leaks every raw array. Degree parsing scans before bounds checks into unterminated leaked buffers, coefficient parsing writes newline rather than NUL and routes arbitrary `NT` through `atof`, and parenthesis parsing returns references to leaked heap temporaries. Arithmetic leaks multiplication/quotient buffers; `divXpoly` performs pseudo-remainder rather than the documented quotient; pseudo-remainder and GCD mutate operands; resultant evaluation indexes the remainder at degree `-1` before testing zero and recursively mutates both inputs. Equality mutates const operands and omits the successful return. Plotting accepts unchecked file indices, zero/negative steps and inverted boxes, narrows sizes, allocates an unbounded two-dimensional VLA on the stack, converts interval midpoints to binary64, clamps out-of-box roots into false boundary segments, and matches branches only by root-order/count continuity. Resultant coordinates are neither paired nor replayed. Hyper's owned exact tensor polynomials, bounded resultants/fibers, typed statuses, and exact curve-topology replay are strictly stronger. |

It cross-checks the same parser/constructor implementation rather than an independent coefficient grid. Hypersolve's exact tensor inputs and Hyperreal's bounded grammar tests cover the relevant boundary without copying this parser. |

Negative powers recurse forever, scalar division can reverse endpoints, and zero-containing division returns that invalid `TOP`. `BiPoly` heap-allocates every inner map but deliberately frees none, retains zero terms/stale degrees, omits stream return, and has shallow implicit copy assignment: mutating an assigned copy changed the source from `x` to `3x`, while Valgrind found 288 bytes leaked in the three-polynomial probe. The shipped interval toys pass only exact-binary cases; the shipped polynomial suite aborts in its malformed negation test because cancellation leaves stored zero terms. More fundamentally, active edge filtering is commented out: an edge is retained using segment distance but its bisectors use the infinite-line polynomial outside the segment's feature zone; the dormant interval-zone predicate itself rejects every projection range wholly inside `(0,1)`. The paper requires conservative interval extensions and JC/MK conditions for every active-feature triple on enlarged boxes. This source ignores the JC scale, checks only its first bisector pair, allows empty bisector sets to certify, omits expanded active-set equality, and falls off `contained_in_polygon()` without returning false. Construction performs no root isolation: it labels four binary64 corners and joins boundary midpoints or the box center; boxes reaching absolute epsilon are colored blue and omitted from construction. Seven of ten stored paper grids visibly contain 2,653--73,118 solid-blue unresolved pixels, so the screenshots document gaps rather than serve as oracles. Five older checked-in inputs repeat their closing vertex, creating a zero-length edge and division by zero under this parser; the `shapes-*` rows also violate the convex-clockwise half-space assumption used by `contains`. The parser accepts unchecked counts/indices and non-finite values, non-positive epsilon can force unbounded subdivision, and the nominal CLI never escapes GLUT cleanup. The main build hard-codes absent `g++-5`/GLUI, the abandoned quadtree headers use misspelled base types, and the optional Bison/Flex parser generates but cannot compile because `bipoly` is undefined and its guard is inconsistent. The official paper explicitly calls the implementation preliminary, machine-double, incomplete in construction, general-position-only, and not yet capable of polygonal sites. Complete manifest SHA-256 is `f1d0fc9a0baa98faefec9e7e2c6a380cfa2aa8cc7d3e3bf63a0e2b23acb51d1e`. No numeric class, topology predicate, parser, fixture, image, or generator transfers. |

The new-thick project independently omits the platform prefix in `"gl.h"`, omits `<ctime>`, generates lowercase `"display.h"` for uppercase `Display.h`, and omits GLU; a diagnostic-only wrapper/include/library correction proves the remaining target links, while compiler warnings independently identify both allocator mismatches. The Windows fork fixes the generated-header case and widget target, but its project links `opengl32`/`glu32` while its timer unconditionally requires POSIX `gettimeofday`; after a diagnostic `<ctime>` injection every translation unit compiles on Linux and linking fails at those Win32-only libraries. Its `greaterThan(QT_MAJOR_VERSION, 5)` condition also excludes the Qt 5 widgets module. All three copied CMake manifests still target absent `2-links.cpp`; current CMake configuration fails exactly there. The nominal 1,680-run WAFR driver is excluded from both projects, trusts every parse/output operation, has no expected paths or warmup/control protocol, and measures the same uncertified planner. The Python converter remains a Python-2, hard-coded Windows-path script. No reproducible correctness oracle, benchmark method, build-size technique, or source-size transfer remains. |

Despite its location in exactCore, this program has no CORE include or exact scalar: active coordinates, interval endpoints, quaternions, footprints, separating tests, and priorities are Eigen `double`/native integers. Its interval arithmetic is not outward-rounded, release assertions can remove domain checks, and determinant expansion is factorial; normalized vectors, square roots, fixed `1e-9`, approximate equality, and heuristic cone/pyramid footprints therefore cannot certify boundary classification. Focused executable counterexamples exposed disjoint intervals reported intersecting, negative-scalar bound reversal, matrix-product shape corruption, a 360-degree rotation becoming 180 degrees, indistinguishable half turns, an undefined identity axis, an inverted triangle separator, stale weakest cache entries, projective-chart division by the wrong coordinate, and tree-code `log2(0)`, negative shifts, and overflow. Retained local repairs make those operations total within their floating contract, use Eigen's quaternion conversion, preserve `q == -q`, and implement the 63-level dyadic comparisons with portable checked `uint64_t` arithmetic. The latter matches arbitrary-precision reference arithmetic over 65,536 exhaustive, 500,000 seeded random, and 49 depth-boundary pairs and passes strict ISO C++14. This is hardening of the audited donor, not evidence of an exact SE(3) planner and not a Hyper scalar transfer. |

## Open candidates

| Candidate | Priority | Required evidence before retention | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Thresholded balanced public `Real` sums exposed by the thesis n-ary-sum measurements | exactness, performance, memory, binary/source size | Preserve exact mathematical results through all owned/borrowed aggregate APIs; retain the old short/unknown-size construction path; independently certify a sum beyond the threshold; test homogeneous exact rationals and heterogeneous symbolics; measure cold end-to-end time, construction-only cost, allocation/peak memory, downstream behavior, and release size. | **retained in `hyperreal`**: iterators whose lower size hint proves at least 256 terms use a streaming binary-carry tree, bounding heterogeneous `Computable::Add` depth logarithmically; short and size-unknown iterators keep the old fold. A homogeneous rational or symbolic prefix collapses in place first. Separate front ends move owned values and keep borrowed short sums clone-free. The initial raw balanced version exposed and then eliminated a 1,024-rational regression from 25.8 to 655.3 us; permanent matched Criterion rows measure 20.71 us sequential versus 16.12 us retained (-22.2%). For 1,024 distinct square roots, construction alone costs 113.14 versus 234.68 us (+107.4%), but cold construction plus binary64 approximation falls from 5.603 to 2.070 ms (-63.1%, 2.71x); 4,096 terms fall about 10x, while the 64-term guard is neutral. A 15-round crossover probe confirms an end-to-end win at the exact threshold: 256 terms fall from 715.9 to 481.8 us (-32.7%), 512 from 1.897 to 0.948 ms (-50.0%), and 1,024 from 5.721 to 1.918 ms (-66.5%). Final paired Criterion rows reproduce the former long-owned clone path at 225.353 us and measure the move-preserving path at 134.630 us (-40.3%). At 1,024/4,096 terms, allocation calls fall 47.0%/60.9%, allocation volume 70.6%/91.1%, and peak live growth 30.1%/71.9%. An independent 384-bit integer-square-root oracle encloses all four public aggregate results for 257 terms; 1,024-term exact-rational and structural symbolic-basis tests cover both homogeneous paths. Default/all-feature Hyperreal matrices pass 602/682 library tests; every target and benchmark smoke in both configurations, formatting, strict Clippy, warning-denied rustdoc, fuzz-bin check, and release build pass. Hyperlattice, Hyperlimit, Hypersolve, and Hypertri pass their default and all-feature matrices. Hypercurve is excluded at the user's direction while its geometry worktree is concurrently edited. A same-path sequential/retained build comparison measures default release rlib growth of 47,578 bytes (+0.68%), text 12,173 (+0.88%), data 16, and BSS zero; public layouts do not change. |
| Global reduce-to-rational expression switch named by `branches/reduceToRational` | exactness, performance, memory, source size | Require a complete linkable implementation; no process-global mutable semantic switch or unsolicited output; exact and total leaf classification; thread/reentrancy coverage; independent value oracles; matched construction/evaluation/allocation benchmarks against Hyperreal's existing canonical folding; retain only a measurable case not already covered more safely. | **rejected as incomplete in the branch and dominated after trunk comparison**: the branch carries the public declaration but omits the flag definition and all `Expr` reduction calls, so a minimal source-linked getter/setter probe fails to link. The later trunk implementation first allocates each operation node, then consults a mutable global flag, recursively extracts a value based on its node type, and prints to standard output for every integer/dyadic/rational collapse. It is neither thread-local nor a general algebraic-cancellation recognizer. Hyperreal already canonicalizes exact rational operations and same symbolic bases without a mutable global mode, and its retained adaptive sum collapse covers the aggregate scaling case with exact oracles and measured time/allocation evidence. No flag, node-type API, debug output, benchmark, or source transfers. |
| Newton reciprocal iteration exposed by `branches/reduceToRational/progs/users/recip.cpp` | exactness, completeness, performance, memory/source size | Require certified nonzero and sign handling, an exact-rational fast path, a seed with a proved enclosure or refinement contract, total zero/nonfinite/precision behavior, an independent residual/value oracle, and matched time/allocation measurements against Hyperreal/Hyperlimit reciprocal construction and Hypersolve use before retaining another algorithm or API. | **rejected as an uncertified demo dominated by current Hyper layers**: the 59-line program converts its input to `machine_double`, seeds `BigFloat` with binary64 `1/a`, applies a fixed user-selected count of `x(2-ax)`, estimates decimal error by converting a BigFloat residual through `Expr`, logarithms, and `doubleValue`, and only prints results. It has no zero check, convergence certificate, precision target, error enclosure, correct-result assertion, or benchmark; an exact-zero residual also makes its logarithmic digit estimate singular. Hyperreal already keeps rational inverses exact and canonical, while Hyperlimit classifies denominator sign/zero through an explicit certification policy before calling the guarded `inverse_ref_assuming_nonzero`; Hypersolve reuses that certified reciprocal path at division sites. Importing this example would weaken exactness and add surface without covering a missing case. No reciprocal code, test, benchmark, or documentation transfers. |

Hypersolve now exposes only `exact_point_witness`; Hypercurve exposes only `represented_exact_point`, `promote_represented_exact_point`, and `promote_represented_exact_endpoints`. The ambiguous public aliases and private rational-named point/vector/coordinate/center helpers were removed or renamed in place. The existing `OnceLock<Option<Real>>` layout is unchanged; `1/pi` exercises public promotion and a source-free `pi` folds as a tensor constant without losing correlation for unmaterialized roots. Four unused recursive-projective target helpers and all 15 inherited lint defects were deleted/simplified without allowances. Parameter/image/exact-Real matrices pass 27/27, 9/9, and 7/7 under all-feature and no-default configurations; strict all-target Clippy, warning-denied rustdoc, both releases, formatting, JSON, and diff gates pass. Ten-run timings are noise-neutral. Matched default/all-feature rlibs shrink 7,658/8,194 bytes to 43,076,812/44,962,270. The rational aggregate's final polynomial-graph replay and broad topology failures remain explicitly open release blockers. Complete evidence is in `hypercurve/benchmarks/checkpoints/2026-09-02-exact-point-payload-alias-reconciliation.json`. |

## Reconciliation checkpoint and current Hyper slices

No unread or unclassified exactCore file remains in either trunk or its sole
branch. Whole-ledger reconciliation reproduced the live branch arithmetic,
replaced opaque old branch digests with a documented path-independent format,
removed stale implementation-pending cross-references, and checked every
retained/rejected candidate against the completed donor record.

Re-reading the retained Hyperreal diff exposed one avoidable long-owned clone.
The final split reducer moves owned terms, preserves the original clone-free
short borrowed fold, and materializes borrowed terms only on the proven-long
path. Threshold, paired Criterion, exact oracle, symbolic-structure, memory,
binary-size, full Hyperreal, and downstream-crate gates all pass; Hypercurve is
explicitly excluded at the user's direction during concurrent editing.

The public cotangent slice is now closed and retained. Its exact rewrites add a
real completeness gain, its checked fallback defines poles and unresolved-zero
semantics without weakening Hyper's bounded certification contract, and its
permanent Criterion/allocation/size evidence exposes rather than hides the
generic-policy overhead. Two more ambitious cotangent kernels were measured
and removed because they made ordinary construction or refinement worse.

The generic expression-sign-filter slice is now closed and retained. A current
Hyperlattice consumer supplied the missing trigger, but exactCore's heuristic
and its irreproducible corpus still did not transfer. The retained filter is a
narrow independently certified interval evaluator with a hard work bound, no
per-node state, explicit precision-floor semantics, measured fallback cost,
and application-level wins. Concurrent Hypercurve CPU work contaminated one
timing sample; the filter-independent construction control exposed it, and
only repeated single-row and alternating same-process measurements are used in
the retained evidence.

The algebraic-zero/root slice is now closed and retained. The implementation is
independently proved, bounded, fail-closed, layout-neutral, serialization-safe,
and materially faster; it resolves all three recorded exact-zero triggers while
leaving ordinary nonzero and unsupported expressions on explicit bounded paths.
The measured source/binary cost is accepted for the exactness and completeness
gain, while degree-ten and mixed-composite extensions were removed after their
measured regressions.

The downstream zero-query slice is now closed. Hyperlattice's checked guards
deliberately reject structural `Unknown` while its ordinary reciprocal already
uses bounded Hyperreal refinement; Hyperlimit and Hypertri consume structural
zero facts only as conservative schedules or metadata. Hypersolve had one
terminal proof hole: a policy-certified exact polynomial remainder was tested
again with structural knowledge only. The retained narrow fix, fail-closed
control, public regression, permanent benchmark, allocation measurement,
release-size comparison, and complete local gates are recorded in the candidate
row above.

The policy-certified nonzero reuse slice is now closed and retained. The final
form keeps owned/structural division hot, enters policy work only after ordinary
construction fails or where provenance is itself the API, and preserves every
unsupported-zero exit. Matched benchmarks rejected eager reciprocal reuse in
bivariate division, established the cold-retry schedule, and found no retained
ordinary regression in the public geometry/image/Krawczyk controls. Allocation,
release-size, complete downstream gates, and shared-fixture source cleanup are
recorded in the candidate row above. Hypercurve remained untouched throughout.

The algebraic-fiber/root-isolation/Bareiss part of the remaining boundary audit
is now closed. Local-field division already retains its divisor proof in the
symbolic quotient representation, and univariate long division already reuses
one policy-certified leading reciprocal and writes the selected leading
cancellation directly. Sparse Bareiss had the actual gap: fixed-bound entry,
fill, pivot, and residual decisions did not continue through strict exact
policy, and construction repeated a proved pivot query. The retained strict
fallback/cold-construction schedule, rejected eager prototype, adversarial
controls, timing, allocation, size, and full gates are recorded in the new
candidate row above.

The resultant coefficient/sign sub-slice is now closed and retained. Every
univariate, typed curve, and rank-independent tensor degree, trimming,

denominators are generated distinct-integer differences or integer factorials.
The exact-`Real` quotient-ring norm's leading source is also already guaranteed

### Hypercurve release continuation, 2026-09-04

- Retained `fec6fc1` on `main`: selected-radial parallel projection now shares
  one immutable system and its exact represented unit-interval schedule. The
  cache distinguishes strict and approximate predicate passes and uses a weak
  target key. The pathological recursive-circle/analytic-parallel regression
  fell from 462.90 s to 162.67 s; the five-test recursive-pair family passed in
  155.51 s. All-feature release Clippy passed.
- Retained `9431c2d` on `main`: zero-distance incidence falls through to the
  authoritative implicit parameter-component solver when the rational shortcut
  has a degenerate resultant or is undecided. All 101 Bezier fit/offset
  integration tests and all-feature release Clippy passed.
- The subsequent complete all-feature release run passed 857 unit tests
  (16 ignored) in 176.48 s and all preceding integration binaries, including
  all 47 curve tests. It stopped at the promotion suite: 90 passed, 10 failed.
  Three failures only assert superseded dispatch paths; seven still concern
  topology, retained certainty, or a fillet reoffset. The two slow direct
  Bezier fillet tests remain performance work. This is not a release pass.
- The clockwise retained-chord expansion failure is reproduced without the
  four newest Hyperreal commits. Exact diagnostics isolated a false crossing
  behind an upward ray from `(-1/2, 2)`: shared tangent ancestry omitted a
  reversed procedural support. The corrected traversal passes a new independent
  exact-segment side/winding comparison over cardinal and oblique directions,
  both traversal orientations, four ancestry depths, and both policies. The
  fix is retained as `9d9a7a5` on `main`; the affected strict promotion
  families pass.
  All temporary diagnostics and the six temporary dependency audit worktrees
  have been removed; their committed source remains recoverable from Git.
- Retained `ba48dd7` on `main`: reversing a clipped or extended procedural
  chord before offsetting now preserves its finite parameter domain instead
  of substituting an ancestral support interval. The new exact-coordinate
  regression checks cardinal and oblique directions, clipping and extension,
  both traversal orientations, signed offsets, and both predicate policies;
  it failed before the fix and passes afterward. The independent nested
  reversal and physical support-intersection regressions also pass.
- Retained `59913ae` on `main`: the promotion suite reached 99 passing cases and one unsupported
  approximate-policy analytic fillet reoffset. Exact predicates must precede
  optional terminal interpretations; failed bounds must not authorize terminal
  equality. The related certainty changes and removal of three passthrough
  Boolean methods pass strict all-target release Clippy, formatting, and the
  new invalid-terminal-bounds regression. A broader run caught a regression
  in an experimental low-level control-hull change; that experiment was
  reverted, and the affected rational measurement and non-miter erosion both
  pass independently. All 29 Boolean integration tests pass after the revert.
  This was not a clean aggregate release result.
- A strict-replay extension for parallel-normal circles did not resolve the
  remaining fillet construction and was removed. Diagnostics localized the
  failure to a nonadjacent selected-circle/procedural-chord pair during offset
  regularization, not to the public certainty label.
- Hypermesh's all-feature release suite passes: 168 unit tests and 54
  integration tests, with seven opt-in competitive tests omitted. Its large
  1,572-triangle / 16,900-crossing exact policy-equality test passes explicitly
  in 83.54 s; no Hypermesh-wide stress or release-completion claim is made.
- The lower-stack heads used in this continuation include Hyperreal
  `8a74b42`, Hyperlimit `7bf9261`, Hyperlattice `d783af0`, Hypertri `6a2092c`,
  and Hypersolve `1a742c6`. Concurrent Hyperreal square-root work remains
  unmodified by this audit; subsequent gates must record the final dependency
  snapshot rather than treating a changing worktree as a fixed benchmark.
- Retained `1c19d68` on `main`: restored all eight rank-independent public
  Boolean cases and both fourth-generation selected-radial offsets to normal
  test discovery. The Boolean matrix passes in 2.77 s release / 27.13 s debug;
  the offset pair passes in 3.20 s release / 22.15 s debug. Removed the obsolete
  six-hour exclusions and corrected the README's explicit stress commands.
- The remaining explicit correctness corpora terminate successfully: the
  genuinely analytic selected-fiber Boolean test passes in 100.97 s, all three
  source-cusp stroke regressions pass in 66.39 s, and both release-scale PCB

  under concurrent work, not controlled speedup claims. The PCB corpus still

- Retained `f5361b3` on `main`: the fillet reoffset is resolved by retaining the
  certified sibling's angular parameter, replay shared/complementary chart
  ownership, then clip in the existing finite parameter domain. This also
  removes an unsound endpoint-only absence inference: a known tangent may
